A novel complex Fermatean fuzzy formalism with improved score function and aggregation operators
Authors: Abdul Razaq, Laiba Komal, Ghaliah Alhamzi, Abdul Wakil Baidar
Published: 2026

ABSTRACT
In the era of intelligent systems and data-driven technologies, making precise and reliable decisions under uncertainty has become increasingly vital. The primary aim of this study is to tackle decision making (DM) issues by developing the technique of aggregation operators (AOs) within a complex Fermatean fuzzy (CFF) environment. A novel score function is defined to resolve the shortcomings of the existing score function for complex Fermatean fuzzy numbers (CFFNs). We introduce two novel ordered weighted aggregation operators: the complex Fermatean fuzzy ordered weighted averaging (CFFOWA) operator and the complex Fermatean fuzzy ordered weighted geometric (CFFOWG) operator. Furthermore, the study explores many important features of the proposed operators. The updated score function and proposed operators (CFFOWA and CFFOWG) are successfully employed to select the most optimal signal processing and communication system for better traffic control. Finally, a comparative analysis is performed to illustrate the reliability and efficacy of the proposed methods relative to existing methodologies. The study contributes to the field of assessment and decision-making by offering innovative and efficient tools and methodologies.

Introduction

Decision making allows organizations and individuals to identify the optimal course of action from a variety of options, thereby ensuring the efficient allocation and utilization of resources and the mitigation of potential risks. The MADM problem is a prominent category of decision-making problems that aims to identify the optimal alternative by considering multiple criteria during the selection process. Aggregation operators (AO) facilitate the identification of solutions to real-world issues and are applicable in a diverse array of fields, such as science, engineering, and numerous others. Aggregation operators are designed to reduce the number of distinct values to a single value by combining them. This guarantees that each value will be considered in the final aggregation result. Crisp sets were frequently employed to aid individuals in decision-making prior to the development of AOs. Conventional mathematical procedures are only applicable in a restricted number of contexts, and membership in a set is not always entirely apparent in real-world scenarios. This is particularly evident in the social sciences, including biology and sociology, as well as in the fields of economics, psychology, and languages. Decision-making often involves uncertainty and imprecision, meaning that exact numbers are not always suitable for ambiguous situations. Zadeh1 introduced the theoretical framework of fuzzy set (FS) for decision science uncertainty. Kahne2 created a method for comparing significant options. FS theory has a vast application in social sciences, production management, and decision-making3–5. Later, it was observed that the membership function alone is not enough to tackle these problems. Atanassov6 defined the intuitionistic fuzzy set (IFS) model, which is an extension of the (FS) theory. An IFS has a membership degree (MD) and a non-membership degree (NMD) such that their sum is less or equal to 1. It can handle ambiguous data in a more effective way. IFSs have performed well in pattern recognition, medical diagnosis, picture segmentation, and fuzzy time series forecasting. Researchers have attained superior aggregation through the use of rules, possibility, probability, and fuzzy logic. Each of these strategies depends on distinct quantitative AOs. Aggregation operators (AOs) are mathematical approaches that reduce a set of values to a single representative measure. Multiple AOs7,8 have been suggested to combine IF information. AOs, including IFWA, IFOWA, IFWG, and IFOWG, had been proposed in9,10. For example, if we encounter a situation where both MD and NMD are 0.6, then the condition 0.6 + 0.6 > 1 indicates that the IFS is incapable of handling this situation.

Yager11,12 offered the Pythagorean fuzzy set (PFS) in 2013 as a solution to the flaws identified in IFS. The squared sum of MD and NMD is constrained to the range [0, 1]. PFSs have the ability to manage a large quantity of unpredictable situations as compared to IFSs. So, PFS outperforms IFS in resolving practical challenges. In addition, many AOs, namely Einstein13, Dombi14, power15, power Bonferroni16, and prioritized17 AOs, have been defined in the framework of PF settings. In 2016, the symmetric Pythagorean fuzzy weighted averaging (PFWA) and Pythagorean fuzzy weighted geometric (PFWG) operators were developed18. The Pythagorean fuzzy ordered weighted averaging (PFOWA) operator has been introduced in19. K. Rahman et al.20 defined the Pythagorean fuzzy ordered weighted geometric (PFOWG) operator. The PFS theory also fails if both MD and NMD are 0.8 because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${0.8}^{2}+{0.8}^{2}>1.$$\end{document}

Senapati and Yager21 established the Fermatean fuzzy set (FFS) theory, to overcome the aforementioned issue, which offers a more comprehensive model in comparison to the current theories of IFS and PFS. According to the FFS theory, the cubic sum of MD and NMD must be within the range of values between 0 and 1. FFSs allow for more flexible modeling of situations because the previous techniques are insufficient for dealing with such problems. Several AOs, likely Hamacher22, Einstein23, Hamacher prioritized24, Yagers25, and Dombi26, have been defined on FFS.

The previously suggested models cannot deal with two-dimensional situations. In 2002, Ramot et al.27 proposed the complex fuzzy set (CFS) by expanding the domain of the membership function from real numbers to complex within the unit disc. These sets have the ability to deal with 2-dimensional data that includes both amplitude and phase components. The theory of CFS has demonstrated remarkable efficacy in resolving a wide range of issues. Alkouri and Salleh28 characterized the concept of CIF set into two groups using polar coordinates expressed as: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le \mathpzc{r}+\mathpzc{q}\le 1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le \theta +\varphi \le 1$$\end{document}. The term complex cubic intuitionistic fuzzy set was demonstrated in29. Multiple evaluations were conducted by various mathematicians in CIF settings, for instance, robust averaging-geometric operators30, novel AOs31, generalized geometric operators32, and Hamacher AOs33. Akram and Naz34 introduced a new decision-making approach under a complex Pythagorean fuzzy environment. This was followed by work on Complex Pythagorean Dombi fuzzy operators by Akram et al.35. The application of such frameworks is demonstrated in selecting the best breed of Horsegram using aggregation operators36. Further developments include prioritized weighted aggregation operators37 and complex Pythagorean fuzzy Yager aggregation operators for MCDM models38. Fermatean fuzzy sets have been defined to deal with MCDM problems in a more effective way39. These modern methods often come from basic principles of aggregation, like the ordered weighted averaging (OWA) operators described by Yager40–42.

Research gaps and motivation of current study

On a closer inspection of the current literature on complex Fermatean fuzzy sets (CFFSs), it can be noted that there are several gaps in the research that are important to be addressed.The score function presented in39 of complex Fermatean fuzzy numbers (CFFNs) is not always reliable compared. As illustrated in Example 1 of this paper, certain CFFNs may provide the same score and accuracy values hence failure to differentiate between alternatives. This weakness reduces the impact of CFF decision-making models.Although many aggregation operators (AOs) have been introduced in intuitionistic, Pythagorean and Fermatean fuzzy context, the ordered weighted aggregation operators within the complex Fermatean fuzzy framework have not been explored. This lack limits the use of CFFSs in multi-attribute decision-making (MADM) situations with two-dimensional data in terms of amplitude and phase-related information.In signal processing and communication systems, complex-valued data often arise naturally rather than being an artificial choice. RF baseband signals are commonly represented as complex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I/Q$$\end{document} streams \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(I+jQ),$$\end{document} preserving magnitude and carrier-relative phase for coherent processing. In coherent remote sensing, SAR Single Look Complex products store complex radar returns with amplitude and phase. MRI reconstruction is routinely performed in the complex domain, where images contain magnitude and phase components. In power systems, synchrophasor measurements are defined as complex quantities with magnitude and phase angle. These examples reflect a common practical requirement: performance is often described by a level component together with a stability/coherence component. Motivated by these measurement conventions, we use complex Fermatean fuzzy modelling to encode two complementary aspects of each evaluation.

As illustrated in Example 1 of this paper, certain CFFNs may provide the same score and accuracy values hence failure to differentiate between alternatives. This weakness reduces the impact of CFF decision-making models.

The score function presented in39 of complex Fermatean fuzzy numbers (CFFNs) is not always reliable compared. As illustrated in Example 1 of this paper, certain CFFNs may provide the same score and accuracy values hence failure to differentiate between alternatives. This weakness reduces the impact of CFF decision-making models.

Although many aggregation operators (AOs) have been introduced in intuitionistic, Pythagorean and Fermatean fuzzy context, the ordered weighted aggregation operators within the complex Fermatean fuzzy framework have not been explored. This lack limits the use of CFFSs in multi-attribute decision-making (MADM) situations with two-dimensional data in terms of amplitude and phase-related information.

Although many aggregation operators (AOs) have been introduced in intuitionistic, Pythagorean and Fermatean fuzzy context, the ordered weighted aggregation operators within the complex Fermatean fuzzy framework have not been explored. This lack limits the use of CFFSs in multi-attribute decision-making (MADM) situations with two-dimensional data in terms of amplitude and phase-related information.

Based on the above research gaps, the proposed research contributes to the analytical ability and practical application of the CFFS framework in the following ways:i.Identifying the drawbacks in the current score function and devising an improved score function to enhance the CFF system.ii.Introducing two novel AOs, CFFOWA and CFFOWG operators, for decision-making scenarios that involve complex Fermatean fuzzy data.iii.Developing a systematic approach for MADM problems using newly proposed strategies, like, CFFOWA and CFFOWG operators.iv.Implementing the newly developed technique for identifying a reliable and efficient Signal processing and communication system for traffic control.v.Establishing a comparative evaluation to demonstrate the accuracy and applicability of recently defined techniques with current strategies.

The remaining portion of this article is organized as follows: Section "Preliminaries" provides a concise summary of fundamental definitions. Section "Enhancement of the existing score function for CFFNs" highlights the shortcomings of the existing score function in the CFF environment and presents a new score function to address these drawbacks. Section "Fundamental properties of CFF aggregation operators" introduces ordered weighted AOs in the framework CFF settings and elaborates on their primary features. Section "Application of Proposed CFFOW Aggregation operator in MADM problem" describes a systematic mathematical procedure to solve MADM issues using the suggested approaches and demonstrates the validity of these approaches by providing a solution to the MADM problem of choosing an efficient technique for signal processing and communication system for better traffic control. It also includes a comparative study demonstrating the efficacy and practicability of this novel strategy in comparison to well-established methods. Section "Conclusions" contains the major findings and broader effects of this study.

Moreover, the degree of hesitancy functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{H}=h\left(\text {\"{u}}\right).{e}^{\k{i} 2\pi \delta (\text {\"{u}})}$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)=\sqrt{1-{{\mathpzc{r}}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{2}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)-{{\mathpzc{q}}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{2}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta (\text {\"{u}})=\sqrt{1-{{\theta }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{2}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)-{{\varphi }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{2}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)}$$\end{document}.

Definition 4.

Ref.21 An FFS \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U$$\end{document} is defined as: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}=\left\{(\text {\"{u}},{\mu }_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right), {\nu }_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)) :\text {\"{u}}\in U\right\},$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mu }_{\mathcal{A}}, {\nu }_{\mathcal{A}}: U\to [\mathrm{0,1}]$$\end{document} represent the membership degree and non-membership degree functions, that satisfy the condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le {{\mu }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)+{{\nu }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)\le 1$$\end{document} for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {\"{u}}\in U$$\end{document}. Additionally, the indeterminacy degree of the FFS, denoted as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\varsigma }_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)$$\end{document}, is defined as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\varsigma }_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)=\sqrt[3]{1-{{\mu }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)-{{\nu }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)}$$\end{document}.

Definition 5:

Ref.39 A CFFS \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} is formally stated as, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}=\left\{\left(\text {\"{u}},{\mu }_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right),{\nu }_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)\right):\text {\"{u}}\in U\right\}$$\end{document}. Within this framework, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mu }_{\mathcal{A}}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\nu }_{\mathcal{A}}$$\end{document} are mappings from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U$$\end{document} to the closed unit disc \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{u: |u| \le 1\}$$\end{document}, defined by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mu }_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)={\mathpzc{r}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\text {\"{u}}){e}^{\k{i} 2\pi {\theta }_{\mathcal{A}}(\text {\"{u}})}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\nu }_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)={\mathpzc{q}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\text {\"{u}}){e}^{\k{i} 2\pi {\varphi }_{\mathcal{A}}(\text {\"{u}})}$$\end{document}, with the constraint that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le {{\mathpzc{r}}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}(\text {\"{u}})+ {{\mathpzc{q}}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)\le 1\text{ and }0\le {{\theta }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}(\text {\"{u}})+ {{\varphi }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}(\text {\"{u}})\le 1$$\end{document}.

Moreover, the term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{H}={h}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right).{e}^{\k{i} 2\pi {\delta }_{\mathcal{A}}(\text {\"{u}})}$$\end{document},

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${h}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)=\sqrt[3]{1-{{\mathpzc{r}}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)-{{\mathpzc{q}}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)}$$\end{document}

and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\delta }_{\mathcal{A}}(\text {\"{u}})=\sqrt[3]{1-{{\theta }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)-{{\varphi }_{\mathcal{A}}}^{3}\left(\text {\"{u}}\right)}$$\end{document} define the hesitancy degree of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {\"{u}}$$\end{document}.

Furthermore, the representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta=\left(\left(\mathpzc{r},\theta \right),(\mathpzc{q},\varphi )\right)$$\end{document} is called complex Fermatean fuzzy number (CFFN).

Definition 6.

Ref.39. Let any three CFFNs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta=\left(\left({\mathpzc{r}}_{\beta},{\theta }_{\beta}),({\mathpzc{q}}_{\beta},{\varphi }_{\beta}\right)\right)$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}=\left({(\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}},{\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}),({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}})\right)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}=\left({(\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}}),({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{2}})\right)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda >0$$\end{document}, satisfy the following operational laws.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\oplus {\beta}_{2}= }_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}+{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}}\right),\\ \left({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{2}}\right)\end{array}\right)$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\otimes {\beta}_{2}= }_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}-{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}}\right)\end{array}\right)$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}^{\lambda }= \left(\left({\mathpzc{r}}_{\beta}^{\lambda },{\theta }_{\beta}^{\lambda }\right), \left(\sqrt[3]{1-{(1-{\mathpzc{q}}_{\beta}^{3})}^{\lambda }},\sqrt[3]{1-{(1-{\varphi }_{\beta}^{3})}^{\lambda }}\right)\right)$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\oplus {\beta}_{2}= \left(\begin{array}{c}\left(\sqrt[3]{{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}+{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}},\sqrt[3]{{\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}+{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}}\right),\\ \left({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{2}}\right)\end{array}\right)$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\oplus {\beta}_{2}= \left(\begin{array}{c}\left(\sqrt[3]{{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}+{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}},\sqrt[3]{{\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}+{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}^{3}{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}}^{3}}\right),\\ \left({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{2}}\right)\end{array}\right)$$\end{document}

Definition 7.

Ref.39 Consider two CFFNs, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}=\left(({\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}},{\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}),({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}})\right)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}=\left(({\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}}),({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{2}})\right)$$\end{document}. The core foundational principles regulating their interconnections are as follows:

i.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}<{\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{2}}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{2}}$$\end{document}ii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}={\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\le {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}\le {\beta}_{1}$$\end{document}iii. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}^{c}=\left(\left({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}}\right),({\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}},{\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}})\right)$$\end{document}

i.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}<{\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{2}}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{2}}$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}<{\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{2}},{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{2}}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\theta }_{{\beta}_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}}<{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{2}}$$\end{document}

ii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}={\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\le {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}\le {\beta}_{1}$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}={\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\le {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}\le {\beta}_{1}$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}^{c}=\left(\left({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{1}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{1}}\right),({\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{1}},{\theta }_{{\beta}_{1}})\right)$$\end{document}

Definition 8.

Ref.39 For any CFFN \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta=\left(\left(\mathpzc{r},\theta \right),\left(\mathpzc{q},\varphi \right)\right)$$\end{document}, the score function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb{S}}$$\end{document} of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta$$\end{document} is described as follows:

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb{S}}\left(\beta\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left[\left({\mathpzc{r}}^{3}-{\mathpzc{q}}^{3}\right)+\left({\theta }^{3}-{\varphi }^{3}\right)\right],$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb{S}}\left(\beta\right)\in [-\mathrm{1,1}]$$\end{document}.

The accuracy function of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta$$\end{document} is described as follows:

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathpzc{a}\mathpzc{c}\left(\beta\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left[\left({\mathpzc{r}}^{3}+{\mathpzc{q}}^{3}\right)+\left({\theta }^{3}+{\varphi }^{3}\right)\right],$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathpzc{a}\mathpzc{c}\left(\beta\right)\in [\mathrm{0,1}]$$\end{document}.

For any two CFFNs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}, {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} , \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} Enhancement of the existing score function for CFFNs

This section demonstrates the inherent drawbacks of the score function for CFFNs introduced in39 and suggests more modifications.

Example 1.

Consider two CFFNs.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}=\left(\left({(0.55)}^\frac{1}{3}, {(0.25)}^\frac{1}{3}\right),\left({(0.35)}^\frac{1}{3}, {(0.2)}^\frac{1}{3}\right)\right)$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}=\left(\left({(0.45)}^\frac{1}{3}, {(0.35)}^\frac{1}{3}\right),\left({(0.35)}^\frac{1}{3}, {(0.25)}^\frac{1}{3}\right)\right)$$\end{document}

The application of Definitions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$8$$\end{document} to CFFNs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} yields the scores \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb{S}}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)={\mathbb{S}}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)=0.125$$\end{document} and the accuracy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathpzc{a}\mathpzc{c}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)=\mathpzc{a}\mathpzc{c}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)=0.675$$\end{document}

Using Definition 8, conclude that the current score function cannot compare \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}.

The refined score function is defined in Definition 9.

Definition 9.

For any CFFN denoted as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta=\left(\left(\mathpzc{r},\theta \right),\left(\mathpzc{q},\varphi \right)\right)$$\end{document}, we formulate the revised score \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}$$\end{document} and accuracy functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}$$\end{document} in the following ways:

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}\left(\beta\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left[\left({\mathpzc{r}}^{3}-{\mathpzc{q}}^{3}\right)+\left({\theta }^{3}-{\varphi }^{3}\right)+{\theta }^{3}{\varphi }^{3}\right],$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}\left(\beta\right) \in [-\mathrm{1,1}]$$\end{document} and.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left(\beta\right)= \frac{1}{2}\left[\left({\mathpzc{r}}^{3}+{\mathpzc{q}}^{3}\right)+\left({\theta }^{3}+{\varphi }^{3}\right)-{\theta }^{3}{\varphi }^{3}\right],$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left(\beta\right)\in [\mathrm{0,1}]$$\end{document}.

For any two CFFNs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}, {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} , \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}$$\end{document} satisfies the following comparison laws:i.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{1}) < \mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{2})$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}< {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document},ii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)>\mathfrak{H}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}> {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document},iii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{1}) = \mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{2})$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\sim {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}. And \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)<\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}< {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document},\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)>\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}> {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)=\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1} \sim {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}.

i.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{1}) < \mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{2})$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}< {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document},

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{1}) < \mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{2})$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}< {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document},

ii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)>\mathfrak{H}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}> {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document},

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)>\mathfrak{H}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}> {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document},

iii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{1}) = \mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{2})$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\sim {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}. And \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)<\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}< {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document},\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)>\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}> {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)=\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1} \sim {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{1}) = \mathfrak{H}({\beta}_{2})$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}\sim {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}. And \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)<\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}< {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document},\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)>\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}> {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)=\mathfrak{C}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document} implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1} \sim {\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}.

The following example shows the effectiveness of the modified score function.

Example 2.

Consider two CFFNs.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}=\left(\left({(0.55)}^\frac{1}{3}, {(0.25)}^\frac{1}{3}\right),\left({(0.35)}^\frac{1}{3}, {(0.2)}^\frac{1}{3}\right)\right)$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}=\left(\left({(0.45)}^\frac{1}{3}, {(0.35)}^\frac{1}{3}\right),\left({(0.35)}^\frac{1}{3}, {(0.25)}^\frac{1}{3}\right)\right)$$\end{document}

Using Definition 9, we obtain CFFNs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}\left({\beta}_{1}\right)=0.150$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak{H}\left({\beta}_{2}\right)=0.168$$\end{document}. Therefore, based on property 1 of Definition 9, we can conclude that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}<{\beta}_{2}$$\end{document}. This indicates that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}$$\end{document} is better than \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}$$\end{document}.

Fundamental properties of CFF aggregation operators

In the following portion, we introduced ordered weighted AOs designed for the CFFNs, namely the CFFOWA operator and CFFOWG operator and investigate their fundamental characteristics.

Remark. Throughout this work, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left(\sigma \left(1\right), \sigma \left(2\right), . . . , \sigma \left(n\right)\right)$$\end{document} indicates the permutation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\mathrm{1,2},3,\dots ,n\}$$\end{document}, so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} -1)}\ge {\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\forall$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\k{i}$$\end{document}.

Definition 10.

Consider a group of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n$$\end{document} numbers of CFFNs \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{\k{i} }=\left(\left({\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\k{i} }},{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\k{i} }}\right),({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\k{i} }}, {\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\k{i} }})\right)$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\k{i} =\mathrm{1,2},\dots , n$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega ={\left({\omega }_{1}, {\omega }_{2},. . .,{\omega }_{n}\right)}^{T }$$\end{document} be associated weight vector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{\k{i} }$$\end{document} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\omega }_{\k{i} }\in \left[\mathrm{0,1}\right]$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\sum }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\omega }_{\k{i} }=1$$\end{document}, then a complex Fermatean fuzzy order weighted averaging (CFFOWA) operator is a mapping \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CFFOWA : {\chi }^{n}\to \chi$$\end{document}, defined by the rule.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CFFOWA\left({\beta}_{1},{\beta}_{2}, . . .,{\beta}_{n}\right)={\oplus }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\omega }_{\k{i} }{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$= \left(\begin{array}{c}\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}}\right),\\ \left( {\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}, {\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}\right)\end{array}\right).$$\end{document}

Theorem 1.

Consider a group of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n$$\end{document} numbers of CFFNs represented as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{\k{i} }=\left({(\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\k{i} }}, {\theta }_{{\beta}_{\k{i} }}\right),({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\k{i} }},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\k{i} }}))$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\k{i} =\mathrm{1,2},\dots ,n$$\end{document}, then aggregated value of these CFFNs through the CFFOWA operator remains a CFFN. This is mathematically expressed as follows:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CFFOWA\left({\beta}_{1},{\beta}_{2}, . . .,{\beta}_{n}\right)$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$= \left(\begin{array}{c}\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}}\right),\\ \left( {\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}, {\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}\right)\end{array}\right)$$\end{document}where, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega = {\left({\omega }_{1}, {\omega }_{2},. . .,{\omega }_{n}\right)}^{T }$$\end{document} is associated weight vector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{\k{i} }$$\end{document} satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\omega }_{\k{i} }\in \left[\mathrm{0,1}\right]$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\sum }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\omega }_{\k{i} }=1$$\end{document}.

Proof.

Theorem proved through mathematical induction. Suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n=2$$\end{document}.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CFFOWA\left({\beta}_{1},{\beta}_{2}\right)={\omega }_{1}.{\beta}_{\sigma (1)}\oplus {\omega }_{2}.{\beta}_{\sigma (2)}$$\end{document}

Breaking down the components:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\omega }_{1}. {\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$=\left(\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{1}}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{1}}}\right),({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{{\omega }_{1}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{{\omega }_{1}})\right)$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\omega }_{2}. {\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$=\left(\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{2}}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{2}}}\right),({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{{\omega }_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{{\omega }_{2}})\right)$$\end{document}

Then,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\omega }_{1}. {\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}\oplus {\omega }_{2}. {\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$=\left(\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{1}}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{1}}}\right),\left({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{{\omega }_{1}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{{\omega }_{1}}\right)\right)$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\oplus \left(\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{2}}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{2}}}\right),({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{{\omega }_{2}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{{\omega }_{2}})\right)$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$=\left(\begin{array}{c}\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{1}}{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{2}}}, \sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{1}}{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{2}}}\right),\\ \left({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma (1)}}^{{\omega }_{1}}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma (2)}}^{{\omega }_{2}}, {\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma (1)}}^{{\omega }_{1}}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma (2)}}^{{\omega }_{2}}\right)\end{array}\right)$$\end{document}

Consequently,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CFFOWA\left({\beta}_{1},{\beta}_{2}\right)$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$=\left(\begin{array}{c}\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{2}{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{2}{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}}\right),\\ \left({\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{2}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }},{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{2}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}\right)\end{array}\right)$$\end{document}

Hence, the validity of the theorem holds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n=2$$\end{document}.

Now, let us assume that the theorem holds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n=\kappa>2$$\end{document}, leading to the following:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CFFOWA\left({\beta}_{1}{,\beta}_{2}, . . .,{\beta}_{\kappa}\right)= {\oplus }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa}{\omega }_{\k{i} }{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CFFOWA\left({\beta}_{1}{,\beta}_{2}, . . .,{\beta}_{\kappa}\right)$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$=\left(\begin{array}{c}\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa}{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa}{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}}\right),\\ \left({\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }},{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}\right)\end{array}\right)$$\end{document}

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n=\kappa+1$$\end{document}, the expression takes the following form:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CFFOWA\left({\beta}_{1}{,\beta}_{2}, . . .,{\beta}_{\kappa},{\beta}_{\kappa+1}\right)$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$={\omega }_{1}.{\beta}_{\sigma \left(1\right)}\oplus {\omega }_{2}.{\beta}_{\sigma \left(2\right)}\oplus . . . \oplus {\omega }_{\kappa}.{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\kappa\right)}\oplus {\omega }_{\kappa+1}.{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\kappa+1\right)}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$=\left(\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa}{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa}{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}}\right),\left({\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }},{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}\right)\right)$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\oplus \left(\left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\kappa+1)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\kappa+1}}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\kappa+1)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\kappa+1}}}\right),\left({\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\kappa+1)}}^{{\omega }_{\kappa+1}},{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\kappa+1)}}^{{\omega }_{\kappa+1}}\right)\right)$$\end{document}

This mean that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CFFOWA\left({\beta}_{1}{,\beta}_{2}, . . .,{\beta}_{\kappa+1}\right)$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$=\left(\begin{array}{c} \left(\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa+1}{\left(1-{\mathpzc{r}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}},\sqrt[3]{1-{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa+1}{\left(1-{\theta }_{{\beta}_{\sigma \left(\k{i} \right)}}^{3}\right)}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}}\right),\\ \left({\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa+1}{\mathpzc{q}}_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }},{\prod }_{\k{i} =1}^{\kappa+1}{\varphi }_{{\beta}_{\sigma (\k{i} )}}^{{\omega }_{\k{i} }}\right)\end{array}\right)$$\end{document}

This proves the validity of theorem for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n=\kappa+1$$\end{document}. Hence, we can deduce that the statement holds for all positive integer values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n$$\end{document}.

The following example demonstrates a practical use of Theorem1.

Example 3.

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{1}=\left(\left(0.8, 0.4\right),(\mathrm{0.6,0.5})\right)$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{2}=\left(\left(0.7, 0.6\right),(\mathrm{0.8,0.7})\right)$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{3}=\left(\left(0.8, 0.7\right),(\mathrm{0.6,0.7})\right)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{4}=\left(\left(0.9, 0.5\right),(\mathrm{0.5,0.8})\right)$$\end{document} be four CFFNs where, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega = {(\mathrm{0.15,0.2,0.25,0.4})}^{T }$$\end{document} be associated weight vector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{\k{i} }$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\k{i} =\mathrm{1,2},\mathrm{3,4}$$\end{document}.
