n$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega = {\left({\omega }_{1}, {\omega }_{2},. . .,{\omega }_{n}\right)}^{T}$$\end{document} be the associated weight vector of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}_{\k{i} }$$\end{document} with the conditions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\omega }_{\k{i} }\in \left[\mathrm{0,1}\right]$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\sum }_{\k{i} =1}^{n}{\omega }_{\k{i} }=1$$\end{document}.

Then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\beta}^{-}\le CFFOWG\left({\beta}_{1}, {\beta}_{2}, . . . , {\beta}_{n}\right)\le {\beta}^{+}$$\end{document}

Proof.

This theorem can be demonstrated by employing the similar logic as in Theorem 3.

Procedure for solving MADM problem using CFFOWA operator

Step 1. Obtain CFF decision matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R=[{{r}_{\k{i} j}]}_{m \times n}$$\end{document} having entries as CFFNs representing alternatives in relation to attributes.

Step 2. To create CFF permuted decision matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${R}_{\sigma }=[{{r}_{\sigma (\k{i} j)}]}_{m \times n}=\left(\left({\mathpzc{r}}_{\sigma (\k{i} j)},{\theta }_{\sigma (\k{i} j)}\right),\left({\mathpzc{q}}_{\sigma (\k{i} j)},{\varphi }_{\sigma (\k{i} j)}\right)\right)$$\end{document}, we follow the two phases outlined below:i.Using Definition 9, find the score values of all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\daleth }_{j}$$\end{document} for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{\k{i} }$$\end{document}.ii.Form the CFF permuted decision matrix by organizing the calculated values of all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\daleth }_{j}$$\end{document} for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{\k{i} }$$\end{document} from the aforementioned phase in decreasing order.

i.Using Definition 9, find the score values of all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\daleth }_{j}$$\end{document} for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{\k{i} }$$\end{document}.

Using Definition 9, find the score values of all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\daleth }_{j}$$\end{document} for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{\k{i} }$$\end{document}.

Illustrative Example

Modern communication systems are designed and operated using signal processing. Making communication better in a smart city, where thousands of devices like traffic sensors, security cameras, and mobile phones send and receive data simultaneously, is an example. Engineering challenges include noise distortion, speed limits, and signal interference. The 5G signal processing systems reduce the delay and distortion through dynamic modulation, error correction, and channel estimation to transmit the data faster, filter unwanted signals, and enhance useful data in crowded regions with high communication43. Base stations and communication towers contain high-powered processors that continuously monitor the conditions of the channel and modify the transmission parameters to achieve the best performance. Emergency communication, self-driving cars, and rapid information collection can never be out of touch due to the more stable, clearer, and consistent signals of this flexible mechanism. Signal processing, therefore, is critical to communication systems that enable information to flow freely in the contemporary digital and connected world. The system should be able to handle numerous signals simultaneously and respond fast. This is to select a signal processing technique that is effective and provides reliable communication despite the environment and channel conditions. The recent research on the intelligent traffic data forecasting and heterogeneous flow modelling also emphasizes the significance of the sophisticated signal processing and communication systems in the contemporary smart transportation systems44,45,46.

An organization intent to upgrade the signal processing and communication system for better control in a certain city. For this purpose, organization hires an expert having a lot of experience in the field of signal processing. His main task is to select an optimal signal processing and communication system to improve the traffic control. In order to accomplish this objective, he selects a set of four signal processing and communication systems (Alternatives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{{\phi}_{1},{\phi}_{2},{\phi}_{3},{\phi}_{4}\}$$\end{document} ).i.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{1}$$\end{document}:Multiple Input Multiple Output (MIMO) systemii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{2}$$\end{document}: Wavelet Transform Techniqueiii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{3}$$\end{document}: Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) based modulationiv.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{4}$$\end{document}: Error Correcting Coding (ECC)

i.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{1}$$\end{document}:Multiple Input Multiple Output (MIMO) system

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{2}$$\end{document}: Wavelet Transform Technique

iii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{3}$$\end{document}: Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) based modulation

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{4}$$\end{document}: Error Correcting Coding (ECC)

In addition, he verifies the efficiency of these systems based on set of four attributes (attributes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{{{\daleth }}_{1},{{\daleth }}_{2},{{\daleth }}_{3},{{\daleth }}_{4}\}$$\end{document}).i.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\daleth }}_{1}$$\end{document}: Signal to noise ratio improvementii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\daleth }}_{2}$$\end{document}: Real-Time data processingiii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\daleth }}_{3}$$\end{document}: Reliabilityiv.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\daleth }}_{4}$$\end{document}: Energy consumption

i.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\daleth }}_{1}$$\end{document}: Signal to noise ratio improvement

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\daleth }}_{1}$$\end{document}: Signal to noise ratio improvement

iii.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\daleth }}_{3}$$\end{document}: Reliability

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\daleth }}_{3}$$\end{document}: Reliability

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\daleth }}_{4}$$\end{document}: Energy consumption

Moreover, he assigns \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega ={\left(\mathrm{0.15,0.2,0.25,0.4}\right)}^{T}$$\end{document} as an associated weight vector of these attributes.

In order to establish a CFFN, these components are later categorized into two unique features as outlined below:Signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) (i) Mean SNR gain, e.g., the average improvement in SNR (in dB) between the output and a reference; (ii) SNR reliability/stability, e.g., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1$$\end{document} minus the outage probability that SNR falls below a specified threshold. These capture overall signal quality and the probability of maintaining it.Real-time data processing (i) Rapid reaction, the physical or operational response delay of the system (e.g., switching/actuation delay); (ii) Immediate data processing, the computational latency required to process incoming data and produce output decisions. Shorter delays indicate better responsiveness.Reliability of signal processing (i) Robustness under network load, quantified through indicators such as packet loss rate or throughput degradation under congestion; (ii) Robustness under varying channel conditions, measured via transmission stability, error rates or interference variation. These two features distinguish resilience to overload from resilience to channel variability.Energy consumption (i) Processing energy, power consumed during signal processing or computation; (ii) Transmission energy, power used for data transmission.

Signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) (i) Mean SNR gain, e.g., the average improvement in SNR (in dB) between the output and a reference; (ii) SNR reliability/stability, e.g., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1$$\end{document} minus the outage probability that SNR falls below a specified threshold. These capture overall signal quality and the probability of maintaining it.

Signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) (i) Mean SNR gain, e.g., the average improvement in SNR (in dB) between the output and a reference; (ii) SNR reliability/stability, e.g., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1$$\end{document} minus the outage probability that SNR falls below a specified threshold. These capture overall signal quality and the probability of maintaining it.

Real-time data processing (i) Rapid reaction, the physical or operational response delay of the system (e.g., switching/actuation delay); (ii) Immediate data processing, the computational latency required to process incoming data and produce output decisions. Shorter delays indicate better responsiveness.

Real-time data processing (i) Rapid reaction, the physical or operational response delay of the system (e.g., switching/actuation delay); (ii) Immediate data processing, the computational latency required to process incoming data and produce output decisions. Shorter delays indicate better responsiveness.

Reliability of signal processing (i) Robustness under network load, quantified through indicators such as packet loss rate or throughput degradation under congestion; (ii) Robustness under varying channel conditions, measured via transmission stability, error rates or interference variation. These two features distinguish resilience to overload from resilience to channel variability.

Reliability of signal processing (i) Robustness under network load, quantified through indicators such as packet loss rate or throughput degradation under congestion; (ii) Robustness under varying channel conditions, measured via transmission stability, error rates or interference variation. These two features distinguish resilience to overload from resilience to channel variability.

Energy consumption (i) Processing energy, power consumed during signal processing or computation; (ii) Transmission energy, power used for data transmission.

Energy consumption (i) Processing energy, power consumed during signal processing or computation; (ii) Transmission energy, power used for data transmission.

In this illustrative example, the two sub-features within each attribute are assigned equal importance for simplicity; however, the framework readily allows unequal sub-feature weights when required. This assumption is adopted only to keep the illustrative example simple; in practical implementations, sub-feature weights can be adjusted based on operational priorities, cost considerations, or expert judgment without affecting the proposed aggregation procedure.

Step 1. The information obtain from the expert is summarized in CFF decision matrix having entries as CFFNs. Step 6. The alternatives’ ranking sequence is determined, indicating that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{1}\succ {\phi}_{3}\succ {\phi}_{4}\succ {\phi}_{2}$$\end{document}. Step 4. The alternative’s ranking sequence is determined, indicating that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\phi}_{1}\succ {\phi}_{3}\succ {\phi}_{4}\succ {\phi}_{2}$$\end{document}. Hence, the MIMO is the most appropriate technique for signal processing and communicating system for better traffic control.

Comparative analysis

Scores and rankings of alternatives under current and proposed techniques.

Table 6 shows that the most desirable alternative identified, determined by the aforesaid procedures, is the same as the one obtained using the suggested operators. This demonstrates the validity and applicability of our suggested strategies for MADM problems. The fundamental principle of our proposed technique is that the CFF setting has a more extensive structure that serves as an expansion of the CIF and CPF settings since it fulfills the criteria. Therefore, this environment is more adaptable for resolving decision-making problems that involve ambiguity. Moreover, one can easily observe that the strategies developed in10,19,20,47 become a special case of the newly proposed techniques in this article.

Conclusions

A novel framework for addressing multi-criteria decision-making problems within a complex Fermatean fuzzy environment has been developed in this study. The limitations in the existing score within the framework of CFFNs have been identified and an improved score function has been formulated to rectify these short comings. Furthermore, two new aggregation operators, the CFFOWA and CFFOWG operators have been introduced, and their essential mathematical properties have also been thoroughly investigated. A systematic methodology has been designed to leverage these operators for solving MCDM problems under uncertainty.

In addition, the newly defined operators and updated score function have effectively been applied to solve MCDM problem of signal processing and communication system. A comparative analysis with existing operators in intuitionistic, Pythagorean, and Fermatean fuzzy settings has been conducted to highlight the advantages and superior performance of our proposed approaches.

For future research, we aim to explore the applicability of these newly defined CFF aggregation operators in other diverse domains such as investment analysis, project ranking, performance evaluation, medical diagnosis, healthcare management, energy resource allocation, inventory control, and transportation systems selection. Extending this framework to include other aggregation operators and developing new decision-making models under complex Fermatean fuzzy sets present promising avenues for further investigation.
